We Officially Ranked the 8 Best Netflix Shows of All Time

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

As one of the biggest entertainment platforms in the world, Netflix has countless original series across practically every genre imaginable. From romance, sci-fi, horror, crime, the streaming giant has built a catalogue that has something for just about everyone. Of course, not every Netflix original is worth the binge, but every once in a while, the platform delivers a show that reminds us just how incredible episodic storytelling can be.

Here, we take a look at the best of the best in Netflix' vast library of shows. These series demonstrate the incredible range of storytelling and showcase great talents in front of and behind the cameras. Some will keep you glued to the screen with unexpected twists, while others will leave you thinking about their characters and themes for days. Spoiler alert: guilty pleasures or "second screen" shows most likely do not make this cut.

Based on John Steinbeck's novel, East of Eden follows the Trask family across multiple generations, starting with brothers Adam (Christopher Abbott) and Charles (Mike Faist) whose fortunes differ when their dad favors one over the other. Their lives changed when the mysterious Cathy Ames (Florence Pugh) arrives at their farm, influencing the decisions they make until later on in the future.

East of Eden is relatively new compared to the other series on this list but it is obvious that this show is one of the best things Netflix has put out in years. Zoe Kazan adapted this classic with care and respect, carrying her grandfather's legacy who adapted the third act of the novel with James Dean over 70 years ago. The thematic exploration of good and evil as well as the generational family dynamics are so satisfying and thorough. The show is further elevated by strong performances from the three leads, particularly Pugh. It also has scene-stealing performances from Hoon Lee, Ciaran Hinds and the new Hunger Games star Joseph Zada. It is undoubtedly one of the best literary adaptations ever made.

Set in the late 1970s, Mindhunter follows FBI agents Holden Ford (Jonathan Groff) and Bill Tench (Holt McCallany) as they research criminal profiling and psychology by interviewing serial killers. Working alongside psychologist Wendy Carr (Anna Torv), the two agents attempt to establish a new investigative discipline by examining the motivations and behaviors of some of the deadliest murderers.

Produced by David Fincher, who also directed seven out of 19 episodes in the show, Mindhunter has become a cult-favorite in Netflix' vast library. The show is moody and well-made, as expected of a Fincher production, but it's also slower-paced than the usual binge-worthy Netflix offerings, making it only a modest success. Anchored by the unlikely yet pleasant chemistry between Groff and McCallany, the show is fascinating for creating thrills out of conversations instead of the conventional crime spectacle. Unfortunately, because of its disappointing performance by the platform's standards, the show ended in its second season. As Fincher has exited Netflix, a continuation is most likely not happening.

In Money Heist or La Casa de Papel, a group of criminals is recruited by the Professor (Alvaro Morte) to carry out an almost impossible heist on the Royal Mint of Spain. The following seasons also see the group, with more additions to the team, back in action targeting the Bank of Spain's gold reserve to secure their lives away from the authorities once and for all. The ensemble cast includes Ursula Corbero, Itziar Ituño, Pedro Alonso, and Najwa Nimri.

With a total of five parts, Money Heist is Netflix's first international hit. The first two parts of the show are actually a re-edited version of the series' original run on a local TV channel in Spain. The striking imagery of Dali masks and red jumpsuits combined with incredible melodrama and intense action make it an immediate audience favorite. Parts three to five were produced by Netflix on a significantly larger budget and they paid off without losing the essence of the previous episodes. The performances from the ensemble cast are so engaging and memorable, resulting in many of the cast starring in high-profile projects afterwards. The series has spawned a Korean remake and a prequel spin-off focusing on Pedro Alonso's Berlin.

Arcane follows sisters Vi (voiced by Hailee Steinfeld) and Powder (Ella Purnell), who become entangled in the tensions in the prosperous city of Piltover and the struggling undercity of Zaun. The sisters eventually find themselves on opposite sides of an escalating conflict involving a new technology, with Powder transforming into the unpredictable Jinx and Vi attempting to reconnect with the person she once knew.

Before 2021, if someone said a show based on the popular game League of Legends is good, we would probably laugh it off. The joke's on us because Arcane is currently the highest-rated Netflix show on IMDb. The show boasts a stunning animation, inventive visual style, and exceptional voicework. The central relationship between the main characters drives the series and makes audiences invested in following their journey until the end. The themes that the show tackle are surprisingly complex and in-depth, examining class divisions and power. Arcane is not only one of Netflix' best shows, but also one of the finest video game adaptations of all time.

Four Netflix characters, four completely different ways of showing up for somebody. This is the one who fits how you actually do friendship, and what having them in your life would be like.

You do not do friendship at half volume, and neither does he. What you want is somebody who says yes to the plan before you have finished describing the plan.

You are the friend who makes things happen, and so is she. Together you would be mildly exhausting to everyone around you and completely delighted with yourselves.

You have no patience for pleasant lies, and she has no ability to tell them. What looks like coldness from the outside is the most reliable friendship on this list.

You look after whoever is having the worst day. So does he. It is the least strategic way to move through the world and the best possible quality in a friend.

The Queen's Gambit follows Beth Harmon (Anya Taylor-Joy), an orphaned chess prodigy who discovers her talent for the game while growing up in an orphanage. After being adopted by a troubled couple, Beth begins competing in chess tournaments, and eventually sets her sights on the international chess scene dominated by Soviet grandmasters. However, Beth also struggles with loneliness and drugs, which hamper her career.

Released during the pandemic lockdown period in 2020, The Queen's Gambit becomes a worldwide phenomenon and even responsible for introducing chess to a brand new generation. Adapted from the book and directed by Scott Frank, the series has a great production design, costumes, and direction. Anya Taylor-Joy shines as Beth who gives a complex performance as the troubled prodigy. She's joined by a strong ensemble cast that includes Moses Ingram, Harry Melling and Bill Camp. With its tightly written story, exceptional performances, elegant presentation, and satisfying character development, The Queen's Gambit is one of Netflix' near-perfect miniseries.

The Haunting of Hill House follows the Crain family, whose lives are changed after spending their childhood in a supposedly haunted mansion where they experienced a series of disturbing supernatural events. Years later, a tragedy brings the family back together and they are forced to confront the memories and traumas they have spent years avoiding. The series stars Victoria Pedretti, Oliver Jackson-Cohen, Henry Thomas, and Carla Gugino.

Created by modern horror master Mike Flanagan, The Haunting of Hill House is not just filled with empty scares. The series is filled with dread that goes beyond jump scares, building tension through unsettling imagery, hidden ghosts, and haunting sound design. It uses horror to explore grief, trauma, and family dysfunction. The series' biggest strength is its emotional storytelling, as each sibling's struggles feel like different responses to the same deeply painful childhood. While every episode is great, a couple of episodes, namely "The Bent-Neck Lady" and "Two Storms", are standouts for their story and technical achievements. Flanagan went on to create more exceptional horror shows, but this remains his best masterpiece.

The Crown chronicles the reign of Queen Elizabeth II, beginning with her marriage to Prince Philip and her unexpected ascension to the throne. Across six seasons, the series explores the royal family's personal relationships and key events that shape the Queen's standing as monarch, from the Suez Crisis to the whole ordeal with Princess Diana. The Queen is played by Claire Foy, Olivia Colman, and Imelda Staunton, with each of them taking the role for two seasons.

The Crown is one of Netflix's best and most fascinating shows because of its ability to turn historical events and political developments into intimate, character-driven drama. Series creator Peter Morgan is no stranger to royal family drama, having written The Queen with Helen Mirren years before. Its outstanding performances by seasoned actors and newcomers, particularly across the show's cast transitions, allow the characters to evolve naturally across decades. It is all further elevated by impeccable production design, costumes, and cinematography that make every season feel remarkably immersive.

Dark begins with the disappearance of a young boy in a small German town, which then brings four families into a generational mystery that spans decades. As the residents search for answers, they uncover a network of secrets involving time travel and the town's nuclear power plant. The story initially is set in 2019, but then jumps backwards and forwards to many timelines. The series stars Louis Hoffman, Lisa Vicari and Oliver Masucci, among many in the ensemble cast.

A convoluted story is often considered a drawback in films and series. However, for Dark, the more complicated the story becomes, the more intriguing and captivating it is. The sprawling, complicated premise created by Baran bo Odar and Jantje Friese is turned into a compelling, emotionally driven story. Its intricate timeline, intergenerational relationships, and constantly shifting revelations demand attention, but the series never relies on easy answers. The series trusts the audience completely to piece together the puzzle. Its gloomy atmosphere, haunting score, and meticulous storytelling become a staple of this show. Few science-fiction series are as ambitious in their construction, and even fewer manage to make a mystery as good as Dark.

A family saga with a supernatural twist, set in a German town where the disappearance of two young children exposes the relationships among four families.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

NYT Connections hints today: Clues, answers for November 25, 2026

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The NYT Connections puzzle today is not too difficult if you're a dog lover.

Connections is the one of the most popular New York Times word games that's captured the public's attention. The game is all about finding the "common threads between words." And just like Wordle, Connections resets after midnight and each new set of words gets trickier and trickier—so we've served up some hints and tips to get you over the hurdle.

If you just want to be told today's puzzle, you can jump to the end of this article for today's Connections solution. But if you'd rather solve it yourself, keep reading for some clues, tips, and strategies to assist you.

The NYT's latest daily word game has become a social media hit. The Times credits associate puzzle editor Wyna Liu with helping to create the new word game and bringing it to the publications' Games section. Connections can be played on both web browsers and mobile devices and require players to group four words that share something in common.

Each puzzle features 16 words and each grouping of words is split into four categories. These sets could comprise of anything from book titles, software, country names, etc. Even though multiple words will seem like they fit together, there's only one correct answer.

If a player gets all four words in a set correct, those words are removed from the board. Guess wrong and it counts as a mistake—players get up to four mistakes until the game ends.

Players can also rearrange and shuffle the board to make spotting connections easier. Additionally, each group is color-coded with yellow being the easiest, followed by green, blue, and purple. Like Wordle, you can share the results with your friends on social media.

U-shaped things: HALF-PIPE, HORSESHOE, NECK PILLOW, PARABOLA

Aston Villa, caso Garnacho: Emery lo esclude e spiega: "I migliori erano qui"

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

C'è un caso Alejandro Garnacho in casa Aston Villa. La sconfitta per 1-0 contro il Galatasaray in Champions League ha lasciato in eredità non soltanto una situazione complicata sul piano dei risultati, con sei partite consecutive senza vittorie tra tutte le competizioni, ma anche un interrogativo sempre più ingombrante sul futuro dell'esterno argentino. Arrivato in estate in prestito dal Chelsea, il classe 2004 è stato nuovamente escluso da Unai Emery, questa volta persino dalla lista dei convocati per la trasferta di Istanbul. Una decisione che assume un significato particolare: il tecnico spagnolo avrebbe potuto inserire un dodicesimo giocatore in panchina, ma ha preferito lasciare un posto vuoto, portando con sé soltanto undici riserve, tra cui due portieri.

Interrogato nel postpartita sull'assenza di Garnacho, Emery non ha cercato giustificazioni particolari, rispondendo in maniera piuttosto netta: "Oggi erano qui i giocatori che ritengo migliori per stare con noi e giocare con noi". Una presa di posizione che alimenta inevitabilmente le voci sulle difficoltà del rapporto tra l'allenatore e l'argentino. Già a settembre il manager dei Villans aveva evidenziato come l'ex Manchester United fosse rimasto indietro rispetto ai compagni a causa delle difficoltà incontrate durante la preparazione estiva. "Garnacho è un po' indietro per alcune circostanze che abbiamo avuto in precampionato. È il momento di avere pazienza e dargli il tempo di cui ha bisogno. Deve continuare a lavorare e, quando avrà a disposizione dei minuti, cercare di giocare bene", aveva spiegato Emery.

I numeri sono emblematici. Da quando è arrivato a Birmingham, Garnacho ha collezionato appena sette presenze, tutte da subentrato, per un totale di 95 minuti. Nessun gol, nessun assist e, soprattutto, nessuna occasione dal primo minuto per dimostrare il proprio valore. L'ultima apparizione risale al 31 ottobre, quando Emery gli ha concesso appena tre minuti nel pareggio per 1-1 contro il Fulham. Nelle ultime cinque partite di Premier League è rimasto fuori dalla lista dei convocati in quattro occasioni.

Personal Consumer Issues • Re: car most similar to a 2014 Chevy Impala--minimal digital gadgetry in the cockpit

i recently rented a 2026 toyota camry hybrid. i didn’t like it, but to be fair, with only 4 busy days of driving to do, i didn’t do a deep dive to figure it out. my brother’s nonhybrid vw jetta s beats the hybrid camry on highway mpg. these points noted, the cpo toyota camry seems like a fine deal, better than a non cpo honda accord for more $ and twice the mileage.

not deal breakers, but for your situational awareness: recent toyota hybrids are spec’d for 0w8 or 16 motor oil, which is way too thin for my tastes, and derives from u.s.g. fuel economy mandates, not necessarily for the mechanical good of the car. in asia the same cars get 0w20 or thicker oil. lots of internet discussion about the oil. also, toyota utuber “care car nut” has noted a brake issue with toyota hybrids.
Is the VW Jetta a TDI? I think they stopped selling the diesels in 2014 though. But I can see why even a nonhybrid jetta using ethanol blended gasoline might also beat a hybrid since it doesn't have the weight and complexity of the hybrid system to tag along at highway speeds.

The 0w-? debate reminds me of the 0w20 debate of 10-15 years ago which had similar concerns. Part of the problem was the dearth of 0w-20 which also cost more. I did note in our 2015 Honda and 2016 Subaru owner's manuals however that they said you could use 5w-20 "temporarily". I used name brand synthetic 0w-20 all the time but haven't tested the engines to high mileage though. I would look at the manuals and wouldn't be surprised if it isn't ok to use 0w-20 with the new cars and maybe even 5w-20. You don't have to optimize EPA testing as the car owner.

Chantiers de l’Atlantique Delivers MSC World Asia

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

The MSC World Asia was delivered by the Chantiers de l’Atlantique shipyard during a ceremony in St. Nazaire this morning.

Now officially part of the MSC Cruises fleet, the LNG-powered vessel is the third in the company’s World-class series.

“Today is an important day for us at MSC that marks another milestone in our partnership with Chantiers de l’Atlantique,” said Pierfrancesco Vago, executive chairman of the Cruise Division of the MSC Group.

He noted that the World Asia is one of the most energy-efficient ships in the world.

“We challenge our teams to go further,” he continued, noting that the company focused not only on sustainability but also on new concepts and enhanced experiences.

Approximately 60 percent of the public venues onboard the 215,000-ton vessel are new or completely reimagined, Vago said.

“Our ambition is simple: to give every guest the best vacation ever,” he added.

Also speaking at the ceremony, Henri Doyer, senior vice president of projects and engineering at Chantiers de l’Atlantique, highlighted the partnership with MSC Cruises.

He noted that while the ship is the third World-class vessel, it also offers new features, including new restaurants and lounges.

Doyer also pointed to the sustainability features of the vessel, which runs on LNG and uses a series of green technologies.

“This has been a shared effort with real collaboration,” said James Moore, senior vice president of newbuilding for MSC Cruises.

“We have built something remarkable,” he added, thanking all partners and players that worked on the project.

The MSC World Asia will now sail to Le Havre, where it is set to be christened ahead of its maiden voyage in the Western Mediterranean.

Spending the winter in Europe, the vessel will offer a series of weeklong cruises to Italy, Spain, Malta and France starting on December 4, 2026.

The seven-night cruises feature visits to Barcelona, Marseille, Genoa, Civitavecchia for Rome, Messina and Valletta, allowing guests to board at multiple ports.

In April 2027, the vessel switches to a slightly different itinerary, which replaces the visit to Civitavecchia with a new call to Naples.

The MSC World Asia will continue to sail in the Western Mediterranean through November 2027, when it is scheduled to cross the Atlantic for the first time.

Sailing from Martinique, Guadeloupe and Barbados, the 5,400-passenger vessel is scheduled to offer weeklong cruises in the Caribbean during the 2027-28 winter season.

With the World Asia now delivered, Chantiers de l’Atlantique’s orderbook includes five other World-class ships due for delivery to MSC by 2031.

The next, the MSC World Atlantic, is scheduled to be delivered by the French shipyard in late 2027 ahead of a winter season in the Caribbean.

Brad Pitt's New $200 Million Crime Movie Officially Makes Netflix History Today

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

For the last thirty-plus years, Brad Pitt has been one of the most prolific and successful actors working in Hollywood. Pitt has broken records and set new milestones along the way, and now he looks set to do so again with his latest highly anticipated film, which releases in theaters today (just in time for the Thanksgiving holiday).

Pitt stars in The Further Mis-Adventures of Cliff Booth, a sequel to Quentin Tarantino’s Once Upon a Time in Hollywood. Tarantino is only in the writer’s chair this time around, with David Fincher (who has worked with Pitt many times before) taking on directorial duties. The Further Mis-Adventures of Cliff Booth is a Netflix film, and is releasing in theaters for a limited run before debuting on the streaming platform in December.

The Further Mis-Adventures of Cliff Booth makes history in many ways. It marks the first collaboration between Tarantino and Fincher, for example, which is especially notable as they are two of the most acclaimed and respected filmmakers working today. However, David Fincher’s new movie also marks a first for Netflix as a studio, too.

The Further Mis-Adventures of Cliff Booth is the first Netflix film to play exclusively in IMAX theaters. Originally, this was supposed to go to Greta Gerwig’s first Narnia film, but Narnia: The Magician's Nephew was delayed until early next year. With the IMAX spot still booked, The Further Mis-Adventures of Cliff Booth stepped right up, and is more than worth the watch in the premium format.

As stated, Netflix has never had a film that has debuted exclusively in IMAX before. The streamer has always had a complicated relationship with movie theaters in general, and, in the past, they have been hesitant to release their films in wide release.

Wake Up Dead Man: A Knives Out Mystery, for example, played in only a very select number of theaters, as Netflix wanted to prioritize its streaming debut instead. That strategy certainly worked (in a way), as Rian Johnson's murder mystery film topped the charts on streaming. Yet, at the same time, some were still upset that they couldn't watch it in a theater, and Brad Pitt's new movie looks set to make up for some of that disappointment.

The Further Mis-Adventures of Cliff Booth will play in many more theaters than Wake Up Dead Man and, not only that, can only be seen in IMAX. Fincher brings his typical auteur style to the new film, making this a truly cinematic experience that deserves to be seen on the biggest and best screen possible.

Audiences can now book their tickets for The Further Mis-Adventures of Cliff Booth, which is playing in a number of major theater chains, including AMC Theaters, Fandango, Atom Tickets, and more. Notably, Regal Cinemas will not be showing the film, and showtimes are available from November 25 onwards.

Audiences also have the chance to watch (or rewatch) The Further Mis-Adventures of Cliff Booth next month from the comfort of their own home. The film will release on Netflix on December 23, just in time for the Christmas holiday. This marks yet another collaboration between David Fincher and Netflix, as the director has also made projects like Mank, The Killer, and Mindhunter for the studio.

The Further Mis-Adventures of Cliff Booth is out now in IMAX theaters and releases on Netflix on December 23.

SpaceX beat third-quarter forecasts with $14.58 billion in revenue and earnings of 35 cents a share.

Apple Black Friday deals: AirPods Pro, MacBook Air, and Apple Watches at new record-low prices

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Apple raised its prices this year, but who didn't? It's something we've seen across the board with tech. Which is why we're all grateful for Black Friday, which is here once again. This is your chance to dodge the price hikes and land a tech bargain. That's why we're committed to paying extra close attention to Black Friday Apple deals.

Yes, Apple does do Black Friday, but not in the way you might expect. Shopping directly on Apple's website usually means getting a gift card for future purchases. That's not quite what most shoppers are seeking. To get the real deals, you need to look at retailers such as Amazon, Walmart, Best Buy, and Target. And as we get closer to Black Friday itself (Nov. 27), retailers duke it out to see who can offer the best prices on AirPods, MacBooks, and iPads. Walmart and Amazon are infamous for outdoing each other by a single cent. Which is fine by us.

Usually, prices on Apple products drop to record lows during the hectic Black Friday weekend. However, especially good deals can also sell out quickly. In years past, Mashable's team spotted many a record low on AirPods only for them to sell out before we could finish writing a 200-word post about it.

Shopping lets you score decent savings while avoiding the post-Thanksgiving chaos. And it's not as if the deals are lacking. Best Buy, Amazon, and Target's official Black Friday sales have technically already started, and Apple prices are showing it. The AirPods Pro 3 dropped to a record low of $149 as of Nov. 25, the Apple Watch Series 12 is $100 and down to a new record low, and the 13-inch MacBook Air dipped below $1,000 for the first time since its price hike earlier this year, but has now popped back up to $1,049. Expect prices to keep nudging up and down like that.

But how do you know what to buy, and what to save for Black Friday and Cyber Monday? That's where the expertise of the Mashable shopping team can be helpful.

Let's start with the very expensive elephant in the room. In June, Apple raised prices on MacBooks, Macs, iPads, the HomePod, Apple TVs, and even the Vision Pro headset, followed by iPhone price increases in September. With some prices rising by $200 or more, the likelihood of them falling to record lows is low. Instead, we research the market to identify products that have been at or near record lows over the past five months.

We also took into account the frequency of deals. If, like the AirPods Pro 3, there's a $50 deal that's nearly always on, we don't necessarily consider that a deal worth highlighting. On the other hand, if an iPad is $100 off for the first time in a month, we'll include it and note its pricing history.

Finally, we considered our reviewers' first-hand experiences with these products. Most of the items in this guide have been hands-on tested by at least one person on the Mashable staff. Our team also prioritized Mashable award winners, choosing the best deals based on overall product performance and value.

We were hoping our favorite AirPods would return to their record-low pricing of $169. Our expectations? Met and surpassed, as our favorite AirPods dropped down to $149, marking a new record-low price by a margin of $20. All told, that cuts an impressive $100 off their $249 list price (a total discount of 40%). Barring any freak price drops (never say never), this will be the absolute best price you can get for these earbuds that come with excellent noise-cancellation, great battery life at eight hours per charge, impressive balanced sound, and a built-in heart rate monitor. The best tech deal this week The Apple iPhone 18 Pro is free right now with T-Mobile

The Apple iPhone 18 Pro is free right now with T-Mobile

Check out our full review of the Apple AirPods Pro 3.

The AirPods 5 haven't received a proper discount yet, as Apple's newest AirPods. The version without a wireless charging case got a few dollars off the $129 price around its release, while the $149 wireless charging case edition hasn't had any price drops. If these buds are going on sale in 2026, Black Friday is likely when.

Though not quite the lowest price we've seen on the 13-inch Air, it's still a great deal considering that post MacBook price-hike, this laptop hasn't fallen below $1,000. According to our laptop expert, this is a great laptop for college students because of its size and zippy M5 power — and it's also an overall daily driver that will more than suit most folks' needs. If you prefer a slightly larger display, the 15-inch Air with the same specs is $300 off at the time of writing.

The M6 Mac mini is on sale for $779 at Amazon, down from $899. That's $120 in savings on the model with 16GB of memory and 256GB of storage. If you already have a monitor, keyboard, and mouse, it's worth a look — just keep in mind that none of those come in the box.

Our sister site PCMag gave the M6 Mac mini an Editors' Choice award, praising its performance, compact design, and ability to stay cool and quiet. It measures just five inches across, but still has room for two front-facing USB-C ports, three Thunderbolt 4 ports, and an HDMI connection, with support for up to three displays.

MacBook prices went up in June of this year, with the Neo jumping by $100 and the Air by $200. That means it's highly unlikely we'll see prices reach their former record lows, even if they start at a much higher price point. I've taken this into account as I select the best deals and note the deals to look out for.

Mashable considers the iPad mini the best iPad for entertainment, making it a strong contender for the best Apple tablet for many shoppers. The up-to-date 2026 version isn't cheap at full price. Luckily, with this $100 off the 2024 edition on Black Friday, the iPad mini is available for exactly that price. The Liquid Retina display is fantastic, as is the zippy performance from the A17 Pro chip. And of course, you can't beat the portability.

The 11-inch iPad Pro is technically on sale, but a $100 price drop on a $1,200 iPad isn't significant. It is the lowest price this tablet has dropped to in the past five months. Still, a $200 price drop would make this a more tempting buy.

The Apple Watch Series 12 was announced just this past September, and we saw it drop to $349.99 once. Then, for early Black Friday sales, it got down to $329. We hoped for more, and we were rewarded with a price drop to $299 the week of Black Friday. Kadyn Proctor can and will play tackle, but Dolphins can wait

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

Miami Dolphins left guard Kadyn Proctor says, of course, that he's willing to do whatever is needed to help the team.

If that means moving out to right tackle, so be it.

But Proctor acknowledges it would be "a little tough" switching sides in-season.

The ideal scenario has the Dolphins listening to this reasonable concern from Proctor and leaving him at left guard until the offseason.

Proctor was drafted 12th overall because he was a standout college tackle who projects as a quality NFL tackle.

If for some reason it were not to work out, we now know he could move back to guard and be a very good guard.

Coach Jeff Hafley conceded this week there have been discussions about Proctor and tackle.

"Conversations have been had, because there's a lot of conversations and I say that to be honest with you," Hafley said.

Those are internal discussions. The Dolphins have not come to Proctor about moving to tackle in the near future. Hafley said he does not envision Proctor playing right tackle against the Jets in Week 12.

"Kind of just focusing and letting me roll at left guard right now," Proctor said.

Proctor has allowed either zero or one sack this season, depending on which evaluation service you choose to trust.

Against the Colts, he was seen overpowering leaping defenders to the ground.

"Once you see that guy in the air, you make sure he pays for it," Proctor said.

General manager Jon-Eric Sullivan got this pick right. Proctor fits in from a size, athleticism, physicality, mindset and culture perspective.

Proctor was asked if he prefers guard or tackle.

Recall that Robert Hunt was once tried at guard and tackle, was good at both, and landed a $100 million contract as a guard.

"I don't have any preference," Proctor said. "Like I said, wherever I'm told to be at, then I'm going to be there and I'm going to play my (butt) off. That's what it is."

The Dolphins are 1-9. If they want to get a 4-, 5- or 6-game look at Proctor at right tackle before the end of the season, there will be no outrage in this space.

Laremy Tunsil, as a Dolphins rookie, played 786 snaps at left guard (we know where he spent the rest of his career) and also 81 snaps at left tackle.

Left guard to left tackle is a bit easier transition for any human than left guard to right tackle.

This conversation is understandable considering right tackle Austin Jackson has struggled and is not under contract for next season. But if Miami wants to have Jackson spend some time watching, they could certainly slide Chu Godrick or Caedan Wallace into the right tackle spot.

Hafley conceded this week that offensive line changes are under consideration.

Right guard Jonah Savaiinaea could always be replaced by Jarrett Kingston, Christian Haynes or DJ Campbell.

Proctor seems likely to be at tackle at the start of the Dolphins' 2027 season.

It's always easier to find a capable guard than a quality tackle. And Proctor's value to the team would be greater as a tackle.

But in an ideal world, Miami allows Proctor eight months of practice reps back at tackle and, and likely, eight months of practice reps in a conversion from the left to right side.

More likely than not he'll excel there, just as he has as a rookie guard.

SXSW Heads To Egypt In Partnership With Youth-Focused UTH Festival

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

SXSW is headed to Egypt through a new partnership with the country’s burgeoning youth-focused UTH Festival, created by Egyptian entrepreneurs Omar El Aawar and Ahmed Ragy in 2024.

Under the agreement formalised at the third UTH Festival, this past weekend in Cairo, the event will serve as SXSW’s partner in Egypt, to connect Egyptian filmmakers, musicians, creators, founders and business leaders to engage with the Austin-based festival’s international network.

In a key element of the accord, an Egyptian delegation, curated and led by UTH, will attend SXSW in Austin, Texas in March 2027. Related Stories News Egyptian Music Star Mohamed Mounir Gets Biopic - Doha Industry Days Festivals SXSW, Deadline & The Improv Launching New Comedy Competition Circuit Breakers For 2027

The delegation is expected to bring together representatives from Egypt’s film and music industries, the creator economy, entrepreneurship, investment and institutional sectors. The organisations also envisage a UTH-led programming session and a community gathering in Austin. Watch on Deadline

“SXSW was founded to help creative people connect, launch new ideas, and shape the future. We are excited to partner with the UTH Festival team, who share our vision to empower the next generation of compelling, visionary and diverse creative voices,” said Peter Lewis, Chief Commercial Officer at SXSW.

“We look forward to working closely with UTH in Egypt, and welcoming leaders from Egypt’s business, entertainment and creative industries to Austin next March.”

The announcement follows a programme at UTH Festival bringing SXSW and Egyptian industry figures together in Cairo. Peter Lewis, SXSW’s Chief Commercial Officer, is attending the festival, with plans for a conversation on the role of international creative festivals and a meeting with selected leaders from Egypt’s entertainment, music and business communities.

“Egypt has no shortage of creative talent. What we need are more opportunities for that talent to connect with the global industry. Our partnership with SXSW is about building that bridge, creating real opportunities for Egyptian filmmakers, musicians, creators and entrepreneurs to take their work beyond our borders,” said El Aawar.

The collaboration also outlines possible Egyptian participation in SXSW EDU and SXSW London in 2027, with programme formats to be developed by the parties. Looking further ahead, UTH’s ambition is to establish an Egypt House at SXSW, providing a gathering place for Egyptian talent, companies and institutional partners in Austin.

The UTH Festival was launched by El Aawar and Ahmed Ragy in 2024 under the banner of Youth Summit, and has continued to double its attendance every year. The third edition unfolded November 20 and 21 at the Grand Egyptian Museum, with the organisers aiming for some 10,000 attendees.

Trevor Noah headlined the event running under the theme of ‘Work in Progress’ and featuring talks across topics of Fashion & Design, Money, Creator Economy, Film & Music, Food, and Sports.

This year’s edition also hosted a short film competition in partnership with Solari, an independent creative production and cultural management company founded and led by CEO Sarah Bissada, attracting approximately 140 submissions from nine countries.

A jury featuring Egyptian writer and director Omar Hilal (Voy! Voy! Voy!); Emirati Maitha Alawadi (X, Charlie) and Egyptian actress Salma Abou Deif (Sheikh Jackson) awarded Egyptian director Abdelrahman Taha the top prize for 7,000 Years Of Color.

‘Ramayana’ Language App Cinedubs Named IFFI Accessibility Partner After Reaching More Than 100,000 Moviegoers in 42 Countries

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The mobile app that let more than 100,000 moviegoers in 42 countries hear “Ramayana” in a language of their choosing has signed on as accessibility partner for the International Film Festival of India (IFFI) 2026 in Goa.

The platform, Cinedubs, has brought its audio description and closed-caption technology to the festival, so that visually impaired and hearing-impaired audiences can watch films in theaters alongside everyone else. Related Stories Agatha Christie Series 'Tommy & Tuppence' Coming to Channel 5 in the U.K.

Nitesh Tiwari’s epic “Ramayana,” starring Ranbir Kapoor as Rama, Sai Pallavi as Sita and Yash as Ravana, opened worldwide on Nov. 5. Cinedubs allowed viewers to pick their own language no matter which version their local cinema was playing, and the company said that service added close to INR40 million ($425,000) to the film’s collections.

The system works through the Cinedubs app. Users download a soundtrack in the language they want and listen through headphones, while the app automatically locks the audio to the film on screen. The same technology carries audio description for visually impaired viewers and closed captions for hearing-impaired viewers.

“Being chosen as the Accessibility Partner for IFFI 2026 is a moment of immense pride for the entire Cinedubs team,” said Aditya Kashyap, founder of Cinedubs. “We firmly believe that cinema is a universal language and that neither linguistic barriers nor disabilities should prevent anyone from experiencing the power of storytelling.”

The company pitches its service to filmmakers, producers and distributors as a way to reach audiences they couldn’t previously get to and to earn extra revenue in the process.

Kashyap said the “Ramayana” numbers validated the company’s goal of making cinema borderless. “Accessibility is not just a social responsibility; it is also a significant opportunity for the global film industry to expand its audience base and unlock new revenue streams,” he said.

During IFFI, Cinedubs plans to meet with filmmakers, production houses, distributors and exhibitors about building a film business that is both more accessible and commercially viable. The company also sees a role in pushing Indian films further into international markets.

Built by Dubswork Mobile Pvt. Ltd., Cinedubs can be downloaded from both Apple’s and Google’s app stores.

For cinemas, the app’s multilingual soundtracks run without any changes to existing projection or sound systems.

Florida Man Jailed for Using AI to Pose as Streamer N3ON in $260K Crypto Scam

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Florida Man Jailed for Using AI to Pose as Streamer N3ON in $260K Crypto Scam

A Port St. Lucie, Florida, man has been sentenced to 47 months in federal prison for using artificial intelligence to impersonate popular streamer N3ON and con a meme coin project's organizers out of roughly $260,000 in crypto.

David Anthony Sanchez, 36, who also went by "Dave the Jewler" and other aliases, was sentenced by U.S. District Judge Melissa Damian after pleading guilty to money laundering and aggravated identity theft, the U.S. Attorney's Office for the Southern District of Florida said on Tuesday. He was also ordered to pay restitution to his victims.

A Port St. Lucie man was sentenced to 47 months in federal prison for using artificial intelligence to impersonate a popular online streamer and deceive investors in a cryptocurrency fraud scheme that caused approximately $260,000 in losses. The case serves as a warning that… pic.twitter.com/4DZnaiaPNt

— US Attorney's Office Southern District of Florida (@USAO_SDFL) November 24, 2026

According to court records, Sanchez set up online accounts made to look as though they belonged to N3ON, and contacted the organizers of a crypto meme coin project while posing as both the streamer and his representative.

He signed agreements using N3ON's identity and sent the organizers an AI-generated voice recording that mimicked the streamer, falsely confirming that N3ON would endorse, promote and take part in the project, prosecutors said. Believing the deal was real, the organizers sent about $260,000 in crypto to accounts Sanchez controlled.

Sanchez then converted much of the crypto into U.S. dollars through transactions designed to hide where the money came from and who owned it, and spent it on himself, according to prosecutors. The press release did not name the meme coin project.

"The defendant exploited artificial intelligence to manipulate the trust of unsuspecting victims and deprive them of their money," Acting U.S. Attorney Yara Lorenzo Klukas said in a statement.

You can now act on the news you read: Go long or short SOL with perpetual futures, all from your Decrypt Money Account.

The case is a small example of a fast-growing type of crypto fraud. Impersonation scams grew more than 1,400% year on year in 2025, driven in part by AI tools, as crypto scam losses reached a record $17 billion, blockchain analytics firm Chainalysis estimated in January.

AI deepfakes of government officials, billionaires and celebrities accounted for 40% of high-value crypto frauds in 2024, according to a report from crypto exchange Bitget. Earlier this month, the Justice Department said North Korean hackers had stolen more than $400 million in crypto over 18 months through a scheme it calls "FaceStealer," which uses recorded webcam footage and deepfake videos to impersonate victims' trusted contacts on video calls. The same tactics have been used to target crypto workers.

Legislation aimed at the problem has stalled. The AI Fraud Deterrence Act, introduced by Reps. Ted Lieu (D-CA) and Neal Dunn (R-FL) in November 2025, would raise penalties for AI-assisted fraud.

AI-assisted money laundering, one of the charges Sanchez pleaded guilty to, would carry fines of up to $1 million or three times the value of the transaction, alongside up to 20 years in prison. The bill has not advanced beyond the House Judiciary Committee.

Is This the Bottom? It May Not Matter. Here's Why!

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

If you're living and dying by whether this is the bottom, something else in your investing plan may need fixing.

By The Premium Team – Nov 25, 2026 at 6:51AM EST

Investors have had a wild few days. On Friday, all three major U.S. indexes closed in correction territory, at least 10% below their recent highs. On Monday, the S&P 500 rose 4.7% and the Nasdaq Composite jumped 6.5%, both their best days since April 2025. The rally came after the board of Nvidia (NVDA -5.86%) reinstated Jensen Huang as CEO. On Tuesday, the S&P 500 slipped 0.5%, its eighth loss in nine sessions, and the Nasdaq fell 1.48%. This morning, S&P 500 futures were down around 1% in pre-market trading, and Nasdaq-100 futures were down 1.61%.

Over the weekend, Motley Fool CEO and co-founder Tom Gardner pulled together a free, live Markets Special Report at short notice to help investors make sense of the sell-off. He was joined by TMF's chief investment officer Andy Cross and analysts from Team Hidden Gems and Team Rule Breakers. During Sunday evening's livestream, a member put that very question to the panel. The market will probably fall further at some point, Andy said. He just didn't know whether that would happen this year.

Team Rule Breakers' Jason Hall turned the question around. He asked members whether they had "structured your financial life so that this is a fun academic exercise." If you're living and dying by whether this is the bottom, he said, something else in your investing plan may need fixing.

Just look at Nvidia. On Sunday night, Huang was out as CEO, and the panel debated whether to buy more shares. Team Hidden Gems' Matt Frankel said the board should at least consider bringing him back. The next morning, it did, and the stock closed up 31%.

Big swings are normal. Andy pointed out that the market has historically fallen about 10% roughly once a year, 15% about every two years and 20% about every five years. It has also risen in about three years out of four.

As one member reminded the panel, quoting investor Peter Lynch, nobody rings a bell at the top or the bottom. So Tom suggests buying in a U shape. Don't try to pick the exact low. Keep adding a little at a time as prices fall and then recover.

A better question is whether your portfolio can cope with whatever comes next. Our investing principles can help.

None of this means the market can't fall further, or for longer. Tom pointed to Japan, where investors who bought at the 1989 peak were still sitting on losses more than 25 years later.

So what would worry the panel more? Matt would be concerned if company earnings slowed sharply. Andy pointed to inflation that won't go away. Jose said he'd watch for AI companies cutting back on data center spending. And Tom flagged signs that sales are getting weaker, such as customers taking longer to pay their bills. We'll be watching all four.

Potential Growth Indicator (PGI): Our PGI compares the cash sitting on the sidelines with the stock market's total value. It stood at 11.47% at the time of writing on Wednesday morning, up from 10.83% on Sunday. That puts it just above its long-run norm of about 11%. Historically, readings above 13% have been good times to buy. Readings near 10% or below point to more speculation.

VIX: The Cboe Volatility Index, or VIX, uses options prices to measure how much the S&P 500 is expected to swing over the next 30 days. It tends to jump when stocks fall. That's why it's often called Wall Street's "fear gauge." It was around 35 after Friday's sell-off. Tom noted that readings above 35 have often been attractive times to add to stocks. By Tuesday's close, it had eased to about 26.

Beta: You'll find this on any stock's TMF ticker page. It shows how volatile a stock is compared with the S&P 500. A stock with a beta of 2 tends to move about twice as much as the market, so expect it to fall harder in a sell-off.

Nobody knows if Friday was the low. This week showed how fast things can change. But if you hold some cash, own a broad mix of stocks and think in five-year stretches, you don't need to know. As Jason put it, the bottom then becomes a fun academic exercise.

When our analyst team has a stock tip, it can pay to listen. After all, Stock Advisor's total average return is 796%* — a market-crushing outperformance compared to 200% for the S&P 500.

They just revealed what they believe are the 10 best stocks for investors to buy right now, available when you join Stock Advisor.

*Stock Advisor returns as of November 25, 2026.

the search for a home without a fixed address, from prefabricated houses to floating dwellings

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

from postwar demountable houses to floating neighborhoods, architects have repeatedly questioned the assumption that buildings must remain tied to one site. the answers reveal different ideas about permanence, ownership, and the future of domestic space.

For decades, architects have imagined homes that can be packed up, transported, and assembled somewhere new, designing alternatives to the idea that housing must be tied to a permanent address. From prefabricated dwellings designed for mass production to experimental houses conceived for remote landscapes, these projects explore how architecture might exist beyond the limits of a fixed site.

One early expression of this ambition came from American architect and inventor Buckminster Fuller. In 1928, he developed the concept for the Dymaxion House, a prefabricated dwelling conceived for mass production and delivery as a single unit by aircraft. Its lightweight aluminum structure reflected Fuller’s interest in applying industrial manufacturing to housing. Although the project never developed into a commercially viable housing system, it proposed a home manufactured away from its eventual site and transported there for assembly.

The idea took a different form in the late 1960s with Finnish architect Matti Suuronen’s Futuro House. Designed as a portable ski cabin, the fiberglass dwelling could be dismantled and transported to remote locations, including by helicopter. Its rounded silhouette and synthetic materials reflected the period’s fascination with space exploration and technological progress.

Mobility, however, does not necessarily mean moving an entire building. It can also involve designing structures that can be taken apart, reassembled, extended, or adapted as needs change. This distinction became particularly important in the work of French architect and designer Jean Prouvé, who explored industrialized construction as a way to make buildings faster and easier to assemble. In the final years of the Second World War, Prouvé developed demountable houses for people whose homes had been destroyed in eastern France. His 8×8 house used a structural system based on a central steel portal frame, with components designed for assembly and disassembly. The project responded to an urgent need for housing, but it also demonstrated how prefabrication could make construction more flexible.

By the 1960s, the question of impermanence had expanded from individual dwellings to the scale of the city. British architectural group Archigram imagined urban environments in which buildings and infrastructure could be continually replaced rather than treated as finished, unchanging compositions. Peter Cook’s 1964 Plug-In City proposed a large structural framework into which housing, offices, shops, and other units could be inserted or removed according to changing needs.

Mobile and temporary architecture is not always driven by a desire for a different lifestyle. In many cases, it responds to an immediate need for shelter. Following the 1994 Rwandan genocide, Japanese architect Shigeru Ban began developing paper-tube structures for refugee accommodation, experimenting with inexpensive, readily available materials that could be used to build temporary homes.

The shelters were designed to be assembled with simple construction methods, making them suitable for situations where building resources were limited. Ban’s work brought architectural thinking into a humanitarian context, showing that even temporary structures require careful consideration of how people live, rather than simply where they sleep.

Another reason to reconsider fixed construction is the instability of the environments in which buildings are placed. Rising water levels, flooding, and pressure on urban land have encouraged architects and communities to explore ways of living on or alongside water. Floating architecture changes the relationship between a structure and its site, allowing the building to respond to water. In Amsterdam, Schoonschip offers an example at the scale of a neighborhood. Developed by its residents with urban planning support from architecture studio Space&Matter, the floating community comprises 30 water plots and 46 homes. Shared infrastructure connects the dwellings, while solar panels, batteries, and a smart grid allow residents to generate and exchange electricity.

Other projects approach the same question at a smaller scale. NOA’s DeKruif House, for example, is a prefabricated timber residence conceived for a family that does not want to commit to one location. Designed to adapt to different waterfront settings, it considers how a house might respond to changing views, orientation, and surroundings. David Rule’s TinyTanic takes mobility onto the water itself, compressing basic domestic functions into a miniature houseboat designed for short journeys. While their scale and ambitions differ, both projects treat the relationship between home and location as something that can be reconsidered.

It is easy to see the appeal of a house that can move. It opens up the possibility of taking a home somewhere new, adapting it to a different setting, or using it for a limited period of time. But moving a building does not automatically make it cheaper, greener, or easier to live in. It still needs materials, energy, water, electricity, and waste systems. A house may be able to travel, but it cannot escape the practical needs of everyday life. These projects also raise a question about why homes are expected to stay in one place in the first place.

From prefabricated houses and temporary shelters to floating neighborhoods, architects have explored different ways of building for changing needs and surroundings. Not every structure needs to be mobile, but more buildings could be designed to adapt, come apart, or be used again rather than remain fixed forever. The Futuro House is a good example of how this idea can come full circle. Designed to be transported and assembled in different locations, it is now a rare piece of architecture that people want to preserve. A house once imagined for life on the move has become something that must stay put. Perhaps the question is not whether we will all live in houses that travel, but whether our homes can change along with the way we live and the places we inhabit.

Australia passes online safety law letting users sue big tech

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The digital duty of care takes effect in a year, with fines of up to 5% of global revenue.

Australians have won the right to sue tech companies over online harm, after the country’s digital duty of care law passed parliament on Wednesday. The Senate approved the bill under a deal Communications Minister Anika Wells struck with the Greens a day earlier.

Instead of regulators chasing harmful content after it spreads, the law makes platforms find and reduce the risks they can see coming before people get hurt. It replaces the mix of industry codes and safety rules that online services follow today, as the Parliamentary Library notes in its analysis of the bill.

Low-risk services fall outside the law, and Wells gave a café or a bookkeeper’s website as examples of sites unlikely to harm their users.

The bill passed the lower house in October with 23 amendments, but needed Greens votes in the Senate after the opposition Coalition came out against it.

The Greens’ votes came at a price: individuals and groups of Australians will be able to sue platforms that breach their duty of care. A separate external complaints body will give people a faster route alongside the courts.

The penalties also grew in the bargain, with companies that breach the duty now facing fines of A$109mn (about €62mn) or up to 5% of global revenue.

Wells said that figure sits between Canada’s 3% and the EU’s 6%. She noted that European courts tend to award only part of the maximum, depending on the case.

For everyday users, the most visible change is a switch to turn off algorithmic feeds, which social media platforms must offer and keep easy to find.

Platforms must also assess the risks of addictive design, such as endless scroll and popularity meters. AI chatbots, meanwhile, will be barred from building emotional intimacy with children.

The law also expands the eSafety regulator’s power to take down nudify apps and adds stronger privacy protections. It also tightens the definition of content that promotes hatred of women. Wells said this targets material that pushes violence and harassment at children, not opinions about feminism.

“If you build it, you should be responsible for its risks,” Wells told reporters in Canberra on Tuesday.

After the vote, Wells called the law in a statement “a landmark safety reform in the vein of compulsory seatbelts”.

The US government warned in a formal submission in September that the plan could push platforms to remove speech to avoid being sued. Labour had already dropped a clause letting the minister add new harms, after critics on both sides warned it could be used for censorship.

The Australian Financial Review reported this week that US ambassador David Brat had criticised the law in social media posts. He argued that vague harms, backed by fines of up to 5% of global revenue, would lead platforms to censor lawful speech. Wells met Brat on Tuesday morning and described the conversation as friendly, with differences still being worked through.

Platforms now get a 12-month transition before the duty takes effect, and the law stacks on top of Australia’s under-16 social media ban, the world’s first, which has been in force since December.
